1d:Tdca,## Summary

Three more serializers mark a value `"[Circular]"` whenever the same object was visited **anywhere earlier in the walk**, not only when it is an ancestor of the current value. Each keeps one `WeakSet` for the whole traversal and never removes an entry, so a graph that references one object from two places (a DAG) is rendered with the second reference replaced by the string. Same class as #25125 (logger), #30994 (JS runtime bridge), #30999 (settings debug).

| Site | Shape | Consumers |
|---|---|---|
| `packages/core/src/runtime/json-output.ts` `stringifyForDiagnostics` | `JSON.stringify` replacer with a global `seen` | `trajectory-recorder.ts`, `trajectory-utils.ts`, `services/evaluator.ts`, experience evaluators, relationships provider. Recorded trajectories and evaluator diagnostics therefore show `"[Circular]"` for a repeated sub-object in a model response or tool result. |
| `packages/ui/src/utils/tts-debug.ts` `serializeTtsDebugDetail` | same replacer shape | TTS debug log lines |
| `plugins/plugin-personal-assistant/src/lifeops/redact-sensitive-data.ts` `redactValue` | recursive walker, `seen.add` without `delete` | currently exercised only by `truncate-suffix-reserve.test.ts` |

## Reproduction (develop `822aba345b67`)

```ts
const shared = { id: 1 };
stringifyForDiagnostics({ a: shared, b: shared });
// → {"a":{"id":1},"b":"[Circular]"}
```

The same input through the other two helpers yields the same shape.

## Expected

Only a reference back to an open ancestor is a cycle. For the two `JSON.stringify` replacers the ancestor path is recoverable from the replacer's `this` (the holder object): pop the stack until the top is the holder, then test membership. For the recursive walker, add before descending and delete in `finally`.


## Consolidated scope — maintainer backlog review, 2026-09-14

Also owns #30999 (settings-debug shared references) and #31113 (log redaction shared references). These are the same ancestor-path versus globally visited-set defect, with distinct output contracts. Candidate PRs are #31005, #31002 and #31119; they are not approved by this consolidation. A coordinated implementation should avoid duplicating incompatible traversal logic, while retaining sink-specific secret redaction, explicit diagnostic preview limits and genuine cycle handling.

Acceptance must include sibling aliases, repeated array entries, true cycles, nested Errors and secret-bearing values at the actual consumers. Check expansion cost on shared graphs. A diagnostic size guard must not silently truncate complete recorded/model-facing content; trace every consumer before calling an output a preview.

The JS runtime bridge data-conversion defect in #30994 stays separate: it changes returned values at a public execution boundary, rather than only diagnostics.


## Logger consolidation and implementation rejection

Also owns #25125, the logger redaction shared-reference report (candidate #30887). Preserve logger-specific secret redaction and log-preview contracts; do not force logger and full trajectory/provider outputs into the same lossy serializer. #31005 adds a 50,000-object truncation marker to stringifyForDiagnostics even though core relationships provider context and recorded trajectories consume it. That implementation is rejected: the replacement must preserve complete model/recorded values or explicitly reject a real resource-boundary overflow without publishing a partial payload. Candidate #30887 requires independent sink review before reuse.
1e:T1547,This change preserves recoverable agent state and exact provider ownership across payment suspension, funded resume, replacement and deletion retries. 20:T49bf,## Summary

This PR consolidates the independently executable duplicate systems identified by the repository-wide audit in #30125. It establishes one executable owner for each migrated behavior, leaves host/platform policy in thin adapters, adds parity or provenance checks around compatibility paths, and preserves the boundaries that are intentionally separate (providers, connectors, auth domains, storage tenancy, platform I/O, generated output, and vendored code).

The highest-risk changes are deliberately structured around canonical kernels rather than broad package mergers:

- runtime/config contracts now execute from `@elizaos/core`, with stable `@elizaos/shared` paths acting as compatibility facades;
- dependency-light config input validation lives in shared, while agent-only defaults are applied in an explicit materialization step;
- LifeOps carve-outs use durable receipts so deleted data is not silently resurrected;
- UI and runtime local-inference selection use the same typed recommendation policy;
- Cloud/local MCP, registry, gateway routing, retry timing, frontmatter, native HTTP, screen capture, host routes, training staging, and quantization now share inward kernels with target-specific adapters;
- data-bearing or native-source duplicates that cannot be deleted safely without live or ABI evidence are guarded and surfaced rather than silently discarded.

Closes #30125.

## Canonical ownership and audit-family mapping

| Audit family | Canonical owner | Adapters / compatibility paths | Contract evidence in this change |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **C1 — runtime/config contracts** | `packages/core/src/contracts/{service-routing,first-run-options,cloud-topology,wallet}.ts` and `packages/core/src/runtime-env.ts` | Existing shared subpaths re-export the core implementations; consumers keep stable imports while executing one implementation | core/shared parity coverage plus broad core, shared, app-core, agent, UI, scenario, and example lanes |
| **C2 — config schemas/defaults** | `packages/shared/src/config/zod-schema.core.ts` owns dependency-light input schemas | `packages/agent/src/config/zod-schema.core.ts` reuses shared schemas and applies runtime defaults via `materializeRuntimeModelDefinition` | shared schema corpus and agent config/first-run coverage |
| **C3 — relationship graph** | Agent `KnowledgeGraphService` and `app_lifeops.life_entities` / `life_relationships_v2` remain canonical | `plugin-relationships` stops treating the retired `app_relationships` schema as an active second owner and adds a read-only `LegacyRelationshipsSchemaAuditService` | `plugins/plugin-relationships/test/legacy-schema-audit.test.ts`; live non-empty installations remain a migration gate, described below |
| **C4 — local inference policy** | `packages/shared/src/local-inference/recommendation.ts` | UI and `plugin-local-inference` project host-specific capabilities/manifests into the shared policy; filesystem/download work remains in the plugin | UI/runtime parity matrix, plugin-local-inference package coverage, shared tests |
| **C5 — copied native sources** | Standalone/native and integrated-fork ownership is declared in `plugins/plugin-local-inference/native/copied-source-ownership.json` | Integrated copies remain build inputs only where the ownership manifest permits them | `native-source-provenance.test.ts` verifies declared paths and hashes; ABI/device proof remains an explicit merge gate |
| **H1 — MCP fork** | `packages/shared/src/mcp/**` owns the edge-safe protocol/schema/naming/provider/resource kernel | `plugin-mcp` retains Node transports and `call_tool`; Cloud retains OAuth and visibility policy but imports the shared kernel | shared facade/schema conformance plus local MCP action/tool-compatibility tests and Cloud MCP tests |
| **H2 — carve-out migrations** | `plugins/plugin-sql/src/carve-out-migration.ts` owns lease, durable receipt, retry, and completion semantics | calendar, finances, goals, inbox, reminders, and scheduling retain domain columns, repairs, and copy callbacks | orchestrator concurrency/failure tests plus each domain migration suite |

| **H6 — screen capture protocol** | `packages/shared/src/contracts/screen-capture.ts` owns the request/image format contract | renderer and `plugin-vision` use the shared schema while retaining native capture versus agent queue responsibilities | shared normalization plus renderer and vision queue/frame correlation tests |
| **H7 — gateway routing** | `packages/cloud/services/_common/src/hash-router.ts` owns EndpointSlice discovery, ring refresh, stale eviction, and target selection | Discord and webhook gateways inject HashRing construction, service credentials, and their logger | deterministic common hash-router tests plus both gateway adapter lanes |
| **H8 — retries/deadlines** | `_common/src/retry.ts`, `_common/src/response-attempts.ts`, and `packages/shared/src/utils/deadline.ts` own retry timing, `Retry-After`, abort/deadline composition, and attempt lifecycle | provider HTTP, proxy fetch, provisioning, and connector paths retain named replay/status/auth policy | deterministic retry/response-attempt/deadline tests, including abort and unsafe replay boundaries |
| **H9 — registry/marketplace** | `packages/registry/src/runtime-kernel.ts` owns wire decoding, normalized entries, cache freshness, search policy, and scoring | agent keeps filesystem/config/network adapters; core keeps install/clone facade | runtime-kernel tests plus agent/core registry suites |
| **H10 — duplicate host controllers** | `packages/shared/src/host-use-cases.ts` owns background-task coalescing and shared pairing-code normalization | agent and app-core preserve auth, origin, rate-limit, persistence, and HTTP translation policy | shared use-case tests and `background-tasks-cross-host.test.ts` plus both host route suites |
| **H11 — frontmatter** | `packages/core/src/markdown/frontmatter.ts` owns bounded YAML/frontmatter document parsing | skills, HookService, agent hook discovery, and skill-learning decode their domain metadata from the core result | adversarial core parser tests plus skills/hook discovery consumers |
| **H12 — training/release pipelines** | `eliza1_staging_kernel.py` and `gguf_k_quant.py` own staging and K-quant mechanics | existing local/real staging commands and public quant wrapper filenames remain compatibility entrypoints with profile data only | staging-kernel tests, real-stager tests, and quant recipe smoke tests |

## Additional consolidation and drift prevention

The same ownership rules are applied to medium-risk exact clones and meta-systems touched by the implementation:

- AES-256-GCM token-at-rest behavior moves to the Node core utility, with finances/health compatibility exports and ciphertext/key-file/environment tests.
- Integration observability becomes a shared event/span contract with agent/app-core policy adapters.
- Voice settings share one UI controller while local/device and Cloud panels keep capability-specific rendering.
- Real-runtime Vitest configuration, native Capacitor/Rollup scaffolding, template cleanup scripts, static assets, and nested workflow templates are generated or manifest-checked instead of hand-copied.
- Cloud migration prefixes gain a total-order manifest and duplicate-prefix check without renaming historical migrations.
- Provider and connector account plumbing gains authoring/conformance helpers while preserving provider/channel implementations.

## Data migration, security, and compatibility

### Relationships

This change does **not** guess that a retired data store is empty. The active graph remains `KnowledgeGraphService`; startup inventory reports whether legacy `app_relationships` tables exist and whether they contain rows. Empty legacy tables may be retired safely. A non-empty inventory is a hard migration decision: rows must be imported with identity, redirect, merge, and evidence preservation before the old schema can be dropped. No destructive DDL is introduced here.

### LifeOps carve-outs

The common migration runner writes a durable `app_eliza_migrations.carve_out_receipts` record keyed by domain migration. It uses a holder token and recoverable lease, marks completion only after the domain callback succeeds, and releases its own lease on failure. Completed receipts prevent a later empty target table from being interpreted as “never migrated,” which is the stale-source resurrection failure this work is intended to close. Domain callbacks remain responsible for explicit columns, validation, repairs, and sensitive-field scrubbing.

### Secrets and untrusted boundaries

- Token encryption preserves the existing AES-256-GCM ciphertext and key discovery contract; adapters do not log plaintext, keys, or provider payloads.
- MCP schemas and model-produced JSON are budgeted and validated in an isolated boundary; Cloud OAuth/tool visibility and local transport authorization remain host policies.
- Registry URLs/wire records are normalized before use; registry cache fallback remains explicit rather than fabricating healthy data.
- Native HTTP and capture contracts validate/normalize crossing data while preserving exact bytes, bodyless statuses, timestamps, dimensions, and formats.
- Gateway discovery retains bounded stale-ring behavior and authenticated Kubernetes discovery; connector identity/auth and forwarded-host policy stay outside the generic hash kernel.
- Retry code centralizes timing and disposal mechanics without granting replay permission to unsafe non-idempotent requests.

## Rollback plan

Rollback is by ownership family, not by deleting user data:

1. Revert an adapter to its prior implementation while leaving new shared kernels present; stable import paths and public wrappers are preserved for this purpose.
2. Do not delete durable carve-out receipts during rollback. Their presence is the safety record that prevents stale legacy rows from being re-imported.
3. Do not drop `app_relationships` on rollback or deployment. If the inventory reports rows, stop and run an evidence-preserving migration before any retirement change.
4. Native integrated copies and public training wrapper commands remain available, so provenance/kernel adoption can be reverted without changing artifact names or ABI inputs.
5. Cloud gateway/retry/MCP rollbacks retain connector-specific auth, OAuth, identity, and routing policy in the host adapters; no credential or tenancy migration is required.

- Merge only after independent review and all required current-head checks are terminal and green.

## Findings that still need explicit implementation/evidence confirmation

The current diff contains clear implementation evidence for every critical/high family at the kernel or guard level, but the following audit acceptance points are **not yet proven by the diff alone** and must remain visible in review:

- **C3:** there is an inventory/guard, but no generic migration can safely be implemented until real installations establish whether `app_relationships` contains data and how identities map.
- **C5:** the ownership manifest and hash/provenance test prevent silent drift, but no diff-only evidence proves ABI symbol parity, scalar/SIMD parity, architecture dispatch, or the exact integrated graph on supported hardware.
- **H4:** shared page exports/frame consolidation is visible, but a single generated `defineAppView` descriptor and a signed-vs-dynamic mount conformance harness are not obvious as standalone artifacts; reviewers should confirm the existing bundle/page tests cover both delivery modes.
- **H6:** the agent/renderer wire contract is unified, but the audit also called for common camera/display recorder and cleanup utilities; no complete cross-native media-recorder extraction is obvious in this diff.
- **H7:** the hash-ring discovery/cache kernel is consolidated. A full shared resolve/wake/forward attempt kernel across gateway servers is not obvious; connector-specific orchestration still needs review against the issue acceptance criteria.
- **H8:** shared retry/deadline primitives and several migrated callers are visible, but repository-wide replacement of every plugin-specific retry loop is neither evident nor desirable without named-policy review. Confirm the intended scope is mechanism convergence, not one universal policy.
- **H10:** background-task coalescing and pairing normalization are shared. A complete transport-neutral first-run transaction/use case is not obvious in the current diff; host-specific first-run behavior should be checked before claiming that subfamily fully consolidated.
- **H12:** staging and quantization kernels/wrappers are present; release artifact inspection with real converter/quantizer binaries remains external evidence.

These are merge gates or intentionally evidence-driven follow-ups, not permission to fabricate success or delete compatibility/data paths.

## Post-open-PR follow-up phase

After this PR is opened and has a stable head:

1. Query every open PR targeting `develop` and classify overlap with the canonical owners above.
2. Post a concise blocking note on PRs that edit a retired implementation or reintroduce a fork, linking to this PR and naming the canonical import/path.
3. Rebase/update affected branches where repository permissions and ownership allow; otherwise leave exact conflict guidance for their authors.

## Reviewer guide

Review by ownership boundary rather than file count:

1. Data safety: relationship inventory and carve-out receipts.
2. Public contracts: core/shared facades, shared schema plus agent materialization, scheduling inputs.
3. User-visible parity: local inference, plugin pages, voice controls.
4. Edge/Cloud kernels: MCP, registry, retry/deadline, gateway routing, host routes.
5. Native protocols: HTTP bytes/statuses and capture request/result semantics.
6. Provenance/meta-systems: native source ownership, training wrappers, generated configs/assets/workflows, migration ordering.

The intended end state is one executable owner per behavior, with compatibility paths reduced to re-exports, generated artifacts, or thin policy adapters—and with no destructive data or source deletion ahead of the evidence needed to make it safe.

# Background

## What does this PR do?

Deployment validation exposed real control and harness failures. Spatial dropdowns were visible to the agent bridge but could not accept its native fill operation; they now use the canonical NativeSelect and report the current selected value. The Goals browser trajectory proves Active and Paused selections change the actual rendered goal list.

Cloud tests on Linux now execute each file in a separate process to avoid the confirmed Bun isolate stdio leak. Server CI runs the process-isolated agent suite on its own runner, with a real planner check proving that the partition retains all selected tasks exactly once. App-control tests resolve the core/errors source subpath correctly.

The repair also removes opaque-sheet GPU work, prevents test imports from resolving into another checkout, and fixes observed test lifecycle and fixture faults: key-specific Vault state, lock-generation assertions, database-clock lease expiry, ANSI-independent CLI output, native dropdown interactions, and route teardown. The sanitizer benchmark compares equal work with interleaved samples while retaining its existing limits. A Node artifact-integrity suite is executed by Node instead of being incorrectly collected by Vitest. Native-library freshness tests create a real isolated Git checkout, preserving the production guard that rejects missing source. Onboarding transport cancellation owns late response bodies; deterministic clock coverage proves cancellation without depending on a one-millisecond scheduling race.

## What kind of change is this?

Bug fixes to deployment validation and the controls exercised by that validation.

23:T13be,Connecting another Google account could change the implicit destination for new built-in calendar links. This change requires an owner-reviewed writable account and calendar, with synchronization paused by default. Durable revision checks and mutation receipts reject stale reviews and prevent destination replacement while a provider outcome is unknown. Rejected remaps preserve existing event identities.

The connections view and registered personal-assistant calendar routes support destination selection, pause/resume, and pending-dispatch recovery. Recovery reads the provider before settling an uncertain receipt. Failures before any external mutation release the receipt; uncertain writes retain it and block replay.

Live school-calendar testing exposed loss of all-day semantics, rejected timed-to-all-day patches, and repeated writes caused by provider timezone normalization. Linked snapshots preserve all-day state in both directions. Google updates explicitly clear incompatible date/dateTime fields. All-day semantic comparison ignores timezone metadata and avoids provider writes when the local and remote event already agree, including older checkpoints.

The production connections view also failed to load because its shared host importer left a bare package specifier in the browser. A literal dynamic import now lets the bundler provide `@elizaos/shared`. The built frontend opens the real pilot connections view, refreshes sync status, and exposes replacement-account controls without browser errors.

Validation at `72e2be875d5707442dc87029988bb1a683e9ce9c`, incorporating fetched develop `d7df7af187f05ec744b57c01799cfbabcc5fd325`:

- Pinned install, root verification, UI typecheck and production web build passed.
- DynamicViewLoader:44 tests passed. App audit:222 capture tests passed;216 findings with zero computed broken/needs-work. OCR:203 verified,0 broken,13 requiring inspection. 24:Tda0,## Behavior

Family coordination now supports monthly email drafts bound to the exact sending account, verified recipient, subject, body, and saved version. Owners can edit the email before approval; each save creates an immutable revision and invalidates stale approval. Failed saves retain unsaved text, and downloaded draft records exclude owner-private claims. The UI distinguishes submission for approval from delivery.

The monthly school workflow supports explicit elementary selection and automatic application of validated changes. Identical PDFs produce quiet no-ops, while source configuration and parser-version changes force reconciliation. Live district-PDF inspection exposed preschool and high-school events incorrectly included for elementary families; this change removes those events from existing imports without recreating valid events. Calendar all-day rendering preserves civil dates across time zones.

Google OAuth configuration reaches the runtime, connection errors remain visible, and verified host-session identity reaches plugin access checks. The connection view supports reviewing a replacement account's permissions and synchronized calendars before retiring a selected test account.

26:Tcdb,Wallet replies now require current evidence for supported operation and holding claims. Prepared or simulated transactions cannot prove submission; an unrelated operation cannot prove a transfer; submission cannot prove settlement. Named-asset quantities, including ordinary no-holdings wording, require observed holdings. Arbitrary address searches can establish queried-wallet holdings but cannot establish personal ownership. Complete request and provider context remains available to the correction model.

28:Td91,## Summary

Fixes #31008. Five data-driven paths issued one database read per list item through `Promise.all`, so their concurrency equalled the size of the sender's relationship graph, the contact list, or the agent's room list; three of them sit under the follow-ups provider, so the bound #30895 adds there was undone one layer down.

- `findEntityByName` (`packages/core/src/entities.ts`): the relationship counterparts are read with one `getEntitiesByIds` call over the deduplicated id set instead of one `getEntityById` per relationship.
- `RelationshipsService.searchContacts({ searchTerm })`: one batched entity read over the candidate contacts.
- `RelationshipsService.getRelationshipInsights`: one batched entity read, then the per-relationship analyses (each re-reads the pair's relationships, room lists, and shared-room messages) are admitted at most four at a time through `mapWithConcurrency`.
- `FollowUpService.getFollowUpSuggestions`: one batched entity read over the candidates and the same four-wide bound on the analyses.
- `AutonomyService.buildEntityNameLookup`: one batched read for every distinct sender per loop tick.
- `plugin-inbox` `fetchChatMessages`: `getRoomsByIds` over the agent's room list instead of `getRoom` per room.

## Stacked on #30895

The first two commits are #30895's exact commits (`git cherry-pick -x`), which add the `mapWithConcurrency` helper this change reuses for the analyses. When #30895 merges, a rebase drops them as patch-identical; the delta of this PR is the commits after them. If #30895 is superseded, the helper commit here stands on its own.

2d:Tf86,## Problem and behavior

A stateful EVM RPC call could be executed again after an ambiguous gateway failure or timeout. A filter poll could consume pending events on its first execution, retry, and return an empty success. With client caching enabled, filter creation/polling and subscription mutations could also return the previous call's result without executing the new operation.

One canonical stateful-method classification now prevents both retries and caching, including mixed batches. Read-only requests retain retries and caching. Every proxy retry caller declares its replay policy. Supplied caller cancellation is preserved before dispatch, during fetch, and during backoff.

This is a bounded current-develop extraction from the RPC family reviewed in #30229, coordinated under #30125. It does not reland that PR's broader retry framework or migration changes, and does not close its umbrella acceptance. Incoming `HandlerContext` does not carry `Request.signal`; cancellation evidence here covers a supplied `retryFetch` signal.

## Evidence

Candidate: `9b952f775fffd7601c4e3a1299074da6307b9a98`  
Base: `3a7f438a9e9d5ba10ea905198723012667bd785d`  
Runtime: Bun1.3.14 / Node24.15.0.

Actual original-production transport reproduction used a local HTTP server, not a fetch mock:

```json
{
  "filterPollAfterAmbiguous502": {
    "providerExecutions": 2,
    "clientStatus": 200,
    "clientResult": []
  },
  "alreadyCanceledRequest": {
    "providerExecutions": 1,
    "clientStatus": 200
  }
}
```

The new transport suite runs the real RPC handler/retry/fetch with only the provider URL redirected to local HTTP. All eight stateful methods, separately and inside mixed batches, execute once after an ambiguous502. Read-only batches retry and return the healthy response. A non-idempotent provider timeout does not replay. Already-canceled, active, and backoff cancellation preserve the original reason and stop further requests.

The cache suite runs the real proxy engine, RPC handler/config, and an isolated instance of the shipped `CacheClient`/`MemoryCacheAdapter` against local HTTP. Authentication, pricing, credits, and usage are fixture collaborators; there are no live financial operations.

```text

  provider executions: expected2, actual1.

Cache regression after fix: 9 pass, 0 fail, 54 assertions, exit0
  Stateful second requests execute and return provider-result-2.
  Read-only balance control still returns provider-result-1 with X-Cache:HIT.
```

2e:T4bb,## Summary

Authenticated Personal Shared pages were POSTing LifeOps activity signals to a Dedicated-only route that returns typed 503 `lifeops_runtime_unavailable`. The renderer already had enough information to stand down, but it retried every 60s and `emitCurrentState` fanned out lifecycle + page-state at the same boundary, producing recurring 503 pairs and console noise.

- Serialize activity-signal sends so only one capability probe can run at a ready/retry boundary; lifecycle/page-state fan-out waits for a successful probe.
- Treat typed `lifeops_runtime_unavailable` (requiredExecutionTier `dedicated-always`) as a document-level latch, not a 60s retry.
- Keep generic 503 backoff for Dedicated plugins that may come up after boot.

Fixes #27898

# Risks

Low. The two utterance callbacks in `TalkModeWeb.speak()` and `stopSpeaking()` change. Callbacks for the utterance that is still current behave exactly as before; only a callback firing for an utterance that `stop()` or a newer `speak()` has already superseded skips the state transitions. Listeners and the returned promise are still resolved in every case.

# Background

## What does this PR do?

`stop()` sets the session idle and cancels synthesis, but the cancelled utterance's `onend` (or `onerror`) fires afterwards and unconditionally moved the session back to `listening` (or `idle` with a "Speech error" detail), resurrecting a torn-down session. The callbacks now check that the utterance is still the current one before touching session state, and `stopSpeaking()`, which also nulls the current utterance while the session stays enabled, sets the resumed listening state itself instead of relying on the cancelled utterance's late end event. Stale callbacks still notify `speakComplete` listeners and resolve the `speak()` promise so no caller hangs.

## What kind of change is this?

Bug fix (non-breaking change which fixes an issue).

# Documentation changes needed?

My changes do not require a change to the project documentation.

# Testing

## Where should a reviewer start?

The `isStale` guard in `speak()` and the listening transition in `stopSpeaking()` in `plugins/plugin-native-talkmode/src/web.ts`, then the four new cases in `web.test.ts`: two drive `stop()` and then fire the cancelled utterance's `onend`/`onerror`, one drives `stopSpeaking()` on a live session and fires the late `onerror`, and one calls `stopSpeaking()` on a session that is off.

## Detailed testing steps

Package suite plus a red run of the new tests against the develop `web.ts`. The red run shows both new cases failing on develop because the session is moved back to listening or into a speech-error state after stop.

# Risks

Low. Test-only change: one new test file, no production code modified.

# Background

## What does this PR do?

Adds unit coverage for `computeContainerBillingPlan` in `packages/cloud/shared/src/lib/services/container-billing-policy.ts` — the pure split policy behind the container-billing cron. Covered rules:

- earnings-first charging with credits covering only the remainder (partial split, credits-only, earnings-only)
- the pay-as-you-go opt-out freezing earnings entirely — including excluding them from the insufficiency check
- combined insufficiency returns `action: "insufficient"` with zero debits
- the exact-availability boundary still bills (strict `<` insufficiency)
- a zero-cost day is a no-op bill, not insufficiency

## What kind of change is this?

Test-only coverage addition (no production change).

## Coverage overlap record (per review finding 3)

`packages/cloud/e2e/tests/monetized-app-loop.spec.ts` already calls `computeContainerBillingPlan` directly for two paths — earnings-only survival (balance 0, earnings ≥ cost → `billed`, `fromEarnings ≥ cost`) and fully-insufficient (balance 0, earnings 0 → `insufficient`). The cron route test (`packages/cloud/api/cron/container-billing/route.test.ts`) mocks the policy, so it does not own the pure-function contract. The incremental cases this suite adds, each preventing a realistic consumer regression at the cron boundary (`packages/cloud/api/cron/container-billing/route.ts`):

| Case | Realistic regression it prevents |
| --- | --- |
| partial split (`fromEarnings` + `fromCredits` > 0) | inverting to credits-first funding silently violates the documented earnings-first rule ("what keeps an earning agent self-funding", module header) and prematurely depletes the org credit balance that the cron's insufficiency warning and other charges depend on |
| opt-out freeze (`payAsYouGoFromEarnings: false` → `fromEarnings: 0`) | an org owner's opt-out being ignored at /cloud/billing, converting redeemable earnings against their explicit setting |
| opt-out excluded from insufficiency check | insufficiency computed with earnings the org opted out of, suppressing the 48-hour shutdown warning an org with zero credits is entitled to |
| exact-availability boundary (`<` not `<=`) | an org with exactly enough combined funds being warned/suspended instead of billed |
| zero-cost day | a free-tier/suspended container day emitting a spurious insufficiency warning |
| full plan-field shape (`totalAvailable`, `earningsEligible`) | the returned policy contract (`ContainerBillingPlan`) that the cron route consumes drifting from the computed decision |

# Documentation changes needed?

My changes do not require a change to the project documentation.

34:T110d,Closes #30949

## Summary

Adds the first owning test suite for the **live** app-update policy copy in
`packages/ui/src/services/app-updates/update-policy.ts`: a deterministic vitest
suite (30 tests) pinning the full platform x build-variant x elizaOS-branch
matrix of `resolveAppUpdatePolicy` (distribution channel, update authority,
auto-update/manual-check/release-notes affordance flags, action labels) and the
install-method authority mapping + status triage of `mapAgentUpdateStatusToSnapshot`.

- No production code changes; one new test file (`+254` lines).
- The parallel `app-core` copy keeps its own suite; no production code imports it.

## Why this test (regression / consumer / ownership)

- **Regression prevented:** `resolveAppUpdatePolicy` decides, per platform and
  build variant, whether the Updates surface offers GitHub self-update, defers
  to a store, or reports "updates unavailable". A silent branch flip (e.g.
  store builds gaining `canAutoUpdate`, or the authority string drifting from
  what the release notes link builder expects) would change update affordances
  for an entire build channel with no failing test today - the ui copy had
  zero tests, and it has already drifted from the app-core twin
  (unknown-authority detail string, tracked in the issue).
- **Consumer:** `packages/ui/src/components/pages/ReleaseCenterView.tsx` - the
  dashboard Updates surface renders directly off these policy fields
  (`actionLabel`, `canAutoUpdate`, `canManualCheck`, `canOpenReleaseNotes`,
  authority detail strings).
- **Why no existing test owns it:** the only update-policy suite in the repo
  covers the `app-core` copy, which no production code imports; a green
  app-core suite cannot catch a regression in the live ui copy (proven by the
  existing authority-string drift between the two).

## Test plan

- [x] `npx vitest run src/services/app-updates/update-policy.test.ts` in

Closes #30916

Definition of Done: full standard in [`CONTRIBUTING.md`](../CONTRIBUTING.md).

# Sync with develop

- [x] Rebased onto the latest `origin/develop` (`git rev-list --left-right --count origin/develop...HEAD` → `0 0`); zero conflicts.
- [x] Focused package gates pass post-sync; the only `verify` blocker is the pre-existing, unrelated `@elizaos/login` type-resolution failure documented under **Known gaps**.

# Risks

Low–medium. `ViewHeader` is a shared primitive rendered by every normal built-in view, so the change is broad by consumer count but narrow in behavior: it restores the header's pre-regression rendering contract. Mitigated by re-running all 19 test files that reference the header (477 tests) plus the focused ViewHeader/Settings/Tasks suites, all green.

# Background

## What does this PR do?

The "Polish view controls" change (`3038df42bf`) gutted the shared `ViewHeader`: its body stopped reading `title`/`onBack`/`backLabel`/`showBack`, rendered only trailing `right` actions inside a `data-testid="view-actions"` div, and returned `null` whenever a caller passed no `right`.

A compact Settings sub-section (below the ~700px breakpoint, including a resized detached macOS Settings window at 390px) passes `onBack`/`backLabel` but **no** `right`, so its "Back to Settings" control disappeared and the next section became unreachable through the UI — the exact regression in #30916. The same gutting silently broke **26 consumer tests** across 9 files (SettingsView, WalletSectionNav, AutomationsFeed, ManagedCloudPage, App normal-page routing, Trajectories, MemoryViewer, workspace mobile sidebar, DesktopSettingsNavigation) that still encode the header's title + icon-only back contract, and it removed the persistent desktop rail's "Back to launcher" control.

This restores the shared contract, scoped to the files that regression touched:

- **`ViewHeader.tsx`** — render the leading chromeless `ViewBackButton` (unless `showBack={false}`), the centered title `<h1>`, and trailing `right` actions, under the documented `data-testid="view-header"` container. The header now renders when a back control is needed even if `right` is empty — that is precisely the compact-Settings case. Section bodies keep their headings visually removed (they use `sr-only`), so **no repeated page heading is reintroduced** — the single header title stays canonical.
- **`DesktopSettingsNavigation.tsx`** — restore the persistent desktop rail's launcher-back control, gated on `onBack` so a detached window that owns its own history still hides it (wide navigation intact).
- **`TasksPageView.tsx`** — remove the now-redundant `FramedPageHeader`. The shell already renders the single "Projects" `ViewHeader` (`App.tsx` builtin-tab header), so restoring `ViewHeader` would have stacked a duplicate title/back row; the view now keeps only its segmented control ("one header per view").
- **`ViewHeader.test.tsx`** — rebuild the full #13451 header contract and add an explicit **#30916 regression block**: a nested section passing `onBack` and no `right` renders + invokes the back control; `showBack={false}` still hides it; trailing actions coexist with the back control.

## What kind of change is this?

Bug fix (non-breaking change which fixes an issue).

# Documentation changes needed?

My changes do not require a change to the project documentation. In-file header/JSDoc comments were updated to describe the restored contract.

# Testing

## Where should a reviewer start?

`packages/ui/src/components/shared/ViewHeader.tsx` and its test. Then `SettingsView.test.tsx` (the issue's named consumer navigation tests).

## Detailed testing steps

```bash

  src/components/pages/SettingsView.test.tsx \
  src/components/settings/DesktopSettingsNavigation.test.tsx \
  src/components/pages/TasksPageView.test.tsx
```

Manual: at a viewport below 700px, open a Settings section → the "Back to Settings" control appears in the header → activating it returns to the Settings list → selecting another section works.

36:T1132,# Relates to

Closes #31004. Same class as #25125 (logger, #30887), #30994 (JS runtime bridge, #30995), and #30999 (settings debug, #31002); this PR takes the three remaining sites found by a repo-wide scan for `"[Circular]"` markers written by a visited set that is never emptied.

- [x] Targets `develop`, branched from `origin/develop` `822aba345b67`; three source files (+37/−14) and three regression cases.

# Risks

Low. Each serializer changes only how it decides a value is circular. The two `JSON.stringify` replacers keep an ancestor stack recovered from the replacer's holder (`this`), which is the standard way to get the open path inside a replacer; the recursive walker deletes the entry in `finally`. Masking, bigint handling, the J4/J7 catch boundaries, and the `"[Circular]"` marker for true cycles are unchanged.

# Background

## What does this PR do?

| Site | Consumers |
|---|---|
| `packages/core/src/runtime/json-output.ts` `stringifyForDiagnostics` | `trajectory-recorder.ts`, `trajectory-utils.ts`, `services/evaluator.ts`, experience evaluators, relationships provider |
| `packages/ui/src/utils/tts-debug.ts` `serializeTtsDebugDetail` | TTS debug log lines |
| `plugins/plugin-personal-assistant/src/lifeops/redact-sensitive-data.ts` `redactValue` | lifeops redaction helper (currently test-exercised) |

Before: `stringifyForDiagnostics({ a: shared, b: shared })` → `{"a":{…},"b":"[Circular]"}`. A model response or tool result that reuses one sub-object was therefore recorded into the trajectory with the second reference replaced by a string. After: every reference renders in full; only a reference back to an open ancestor collapses.

## What kind of change is this?

Bug fix.

# Documentation changes needed?

None.

# Testing

## Where should a reviewer start?

`packages/core/src/runtime/json-output.ts` (the replacer in `stringifyForDiagnostics`), then `json-output.diagnostics.test.ts`; the other two sites are the same shape.

## Detailed testing steps

At head `1b178b488d48` (node budgets added after review): core json-output.diagnostics 3/3 + __tests__/json-output 22/22, ui tts-debug 13/13, personal-assistant redact-sensitive-data 3/3 + truncate-suffix-reserve 6/6, core tsc 0 errors, Biome clean.

Earlier head:

RED on develop `822aba345b67` with the new cases and the unchanged serializers, one per package:

```text
core  × renders a shared (non-cyclic) reference in full at every site        (1 failed | 1 passed)
ui    × renders a shared (non-cyclic) diagnostic value in full at every site (1 failed | 11 passed)
pa    × redacts a shared (non-cyclic) reference in full at every site        (1 failed | 1 passed)
```

The true-cycle cases pass on both sides, pinning that `"[Circular]"` still appears for a real cycle and that the cycle's siblings still serialize.

With the fix:

# Risks

Low. Change is bounded to `plugins/plugin-mcp` (`utils/processing.ts`, `actions/mcp.ts`, `prompts.ts`). The only behavior change is on the MCP `call_tool` path when the server returns a spec-compliant errored `CallToolResult` (`isError: true`): that call now returns `ActionResult.success:false` instead of a fabricated success. The success path is unchanged (regression-tested). No public type is removed — `isError` was already declared in `types.ts` and `processing.ts`; this PR makes it actually consumed.

# Background

## What does this PR do?

`CALL_MCP_TOOL` never read the SDK's `CallToolResult.isError` flag. When an MCP server returned a structured error result (`isError: true` with the error text in `content` — the spec-mandated way tools report execution failures), `processToolResult()` merged the error text into `toolOutput` indistinguishably from a success payload, and the action returned:

```
{ text: "Successfully called tool: <server>/<tool>", values: { success: true, toolExecuted: true, ... }, success: true }
```

So a tool that failed (upstream 500, invalid API key, …) was recorded as a **successful** tool execution and the agent narrated the error content to the user as if it were data. This violates the repo error policy ("Do not catch and continue with fabricated success"; "Action/tool failures already return to the planner path"). `isError` was declared in `types.ts` and `processing.ts` but read nowhere in the package.

The fix:

- `processToolResult()` now returns `isError: result.isError === true` alongside `toolOutput`/attachments.
- `handleCallTool()` branches on `isError`: an errored result returns a distinct `ActionResult` with `success:false`, `values.toolErrored:true`, `data.isError:true`, a typed `McpError("…TOOL_EXECUTION_ERROR")`, and text `"Tool <server>/<tool> reported an error…"` — never `"Successfully called tool"`.
- The user is still informed: `handleToolResponse()` threads a `toolErrored` hint into the reasoning template so the model frames the outcome as a failure rather than presenting the error content as requested data.

## What kind of change is this?

Bug fix (non-breaking; fixes fabricated success on the planner path).

# Documentation changes needed?

No. Behavior is corrected to match the MCP spec and the repo error policy; no user-facing docs describe the previous (incorrect) behavior.

# Testing

## Where should a reviewer start?

`plugins/plugin-mcp/src/utils/__tests__/processing.test.ts` (pure `processToolResult` error-signal cases) and the new `plugins/plugin-mcp/src/actions/__tests__/mcp-call-tool-error.test.ts` (real `mcpAction.handler` with a stub `McpService.callTool` returning `isError:true`).

## Detailed testing steps

```bash
cd plugins/plugin-mcp
npx vitest run --config vitest.config.ts \
  src/utils/__tests__/processing.test.ts \
  src/actions/__tests__/mcp-call-tool-error.test.ts
```

The action test asserts: errored result → `result.success === false`, `values.toolErrored === true`, `result.error instanceof Error`, text no longer matches `/Successfully called tool/`; success result → `result.success === true`, text still claims success; and that the reasoning prompt for an errored call contains the failure framing.

### Before / After (reproduction artifact)

<details><summary>isError dropped → surfaced + grep proof isError is now consumed</summary>

```
## BEFORE (origin/develop) — isError silently dropped
plugins/plugin-mcp/src/utils/processing.ts:125:
  return { toolOutput, hasAttachments, attachments };
=> Object.keys(out) === ['toolOutput','hasAttachments','attachments']; out.isError === undefined
=> actions/mcp.ts returned success:true / toolExecuted:true / "Successfully called tool"
   even when the server set isError:true.

## AFTER (this branch) — isError surfaced and honored
```

# Risks

Low. The change is confined to `McpToolCompatibility.processSchema` dispatch in `plugins/plugin-mcp/src/tool-compatibility/base.ts`. Scalar-typed schema nodes take the identical code path as before; only array-valued `type` nodes (previously unhandled) gain per-type cleaning. No public surface, export, or type changed.

# Background

## What does this PR do?

`McpToolCompatibility.processSchema` dispatched on a string `type` switch (`"string"/"number"/"integer"/"array"/"object"`). JSON Schema also permits `type` to be an **array** of types — e.g. `type: ["string","null"]`, the standard nullable/optional encoding emitted by Zod `.nullable()` and by many MCP servers. Such nodes matched no case and fell through to `processGenericSchema`, which only walks `oneOf/anyOf/allOf`: it stripped **no** per-type validation keywords and never recursed into `properties`/`items`.

The whole purpose of this layer is to remove keywords that strict provider function-calling schemas reject. So for any nullable field — and everything nested under a nullable object/array — provider-unsupported keywords (`format`, `pattern`, `minLength/maxLength`, `minimum/maximum`, `minItems`, …) survived. On Google/Gemini (a strict OpenAPI subset that 400s on unsupported `format` and keywords) and OpenAI strict/reasoning modes, `callTool` then failed at the provider boundary even though the compatibility fixup had run.

The fix routes array-typed nodes through a new `processUnionTypeSchema`, which applies each concrete per-type processor the node declares — stripping the matching keywords, folding them into the `description` (preserving existing description-folding behavior), and recursing into `properties`/`items` — while preserving the original nullable `type` array on the output. It then runs the combinator handling so `oneOf/anyOf/allOf` behavior stays identical to the scalar path. `"null"` and `"boolean"` members carry no strippable keywords and are skipped.

## What kind of change is this?

Bug fix (non-breaking change which fixes an issue).

# Documentation changes needed?

My changes do not require a change to the project documentation. Behavior is corrected to match the layer's documented purpose (strip provider-unsupported keywords); no public API or documented contract changed.

# Testing

## Where should a reviewer start?

`plugins/plugin-mcp/__tests__/tool-compatibility.test.ts` → the new `nullable (union) typed nodes` describe block. Each case asserts the fixed contract against a real `GoogleMcpCompatibility` / `OpenAIReasoningMcpCompatibility` instance with deterministic JSON Schema input and no external MCP server.

3d:Tdcb,# Relates to

Closes #30994.

- [x] Targets `develop`, branched from `origin/develop` `822aba345b67`; two files, +70/−9.

# Risks

Low. The change is confined to `marshalValue` in `packages/agent/src/services/js-runtime-bridge.ts`: the visited set becomes an ancestor-path set (add before descending, delete in `finally`). True cycles still collapse to `"[cycle]"`; shared references now marshal in full. No wire-shape change.

# Background

## What does this PR do?

`toJsValue` shared one `seen` set across every sibling and never removed an object after its subtree was marshalled, so any object reached twice through different paths came back as the string `"[cycle]"`. Both `HostNodeBridge.evaluate` (script results) and `importModule` (module exports) route through it, so a plugin export graph that reuses an object (a config referenced from two keys, the same array in two places) reached the host with the second reference replaced by a string. Same defect class as the logger walker fixed in #25125.

Now an object is on the set only while its own children are being marshalled. A reference back to an open ancestor is the only thing that collapses to `"[cycle]"`.

## What kind of change is this?

Bug fix.

# Documentation changes needed?

None; the `toJsValue` docblock now states the ancestor-only cycle rule.

# Testing

## Where should a reviewer start?

`packages/agent/src/services/js-runtime-bridge.ts` (`marshalValue`), then the two new cases in `js-runtime-bridge.test.ts`, which go through the real host-node bridge (`node:vm`) rather than calling the marshaller directly.

Closes #30996.

- [x] Targets `develop`, branched from `origin/develop` `822aba345b67`; three files, +25/−12 in the service and its test mock plus a new regression suite.

# Risks

Low. The service constructor reads `SKILLS_SYNC_CATALOG_ON_START`, `SKILLS_AUTO_LOAD` / `CLAWHUB_AUTO_LOAD`, and `SKILLS_AUTO_REFRESH` through core's `parseBooleanValue` instead of comparing to `"true"` / `"false"`. Defaults are unchanged (`false`, `true`, `false`); the string spellings behave as before, and `"1"`/`"yes"`/`"on"` and their negatives are now accepted the way every other boolean setting in core is.

# Background

## What does this PR do?

`getSetting` returns `string | boolean | number | null` and the plugin manifest declares `SKILLS_AUTO_LOAD` as a boolean, so a settings UI stores a real boolean. `false !== "false"` is true, so the documented off switch did nothing; `true === "true"` is false, so auto-refresh could not be turned on. The same constructor already accepted both forms for `SKILLS_SYNC_CATALOG_ON_START`. All three flags now go through one parser.

## What kind of change is this?

Bug fix.

# Documentation changes needed?

None. The settings table in the plugin guides names the flags and defaults, which are unchanged.

42:T1fa9,Follow-through on #30830 (merged as `24a3bb1469`). That PR made both playback backends retire crossfaded samples, old-only samples, and discarded handoff tails from queue depth, so telemetry stops reporting audio that can no longer play. Two of the four accounting lines it added per backend are unpinned by the merged suites: the old-only retirement, and the retirement of the tail abandoned when a handoff is replaced before its crossfade completes. Test-only; no runtime source is touched.

## What was unpinned (reproduced on clean develop before the fix)

| mutant (line deleted) | develop suite | with this PR |
| --- | --- | --- |
| ScriptProcessor: `consumedSamples += 1` in the old-only branch (`voice-session-playback.ts`) | 29 pass | **1 failed** |
| ScriptProcessor: `sinkQueuedSamples -= …` on `beginHandoff` (abandoned tail) | 29 pass | **1 failed** |
| AudioWorklet: `queuedSamples - 1` in the old-only branch (`voice-session-downlink.js`) | 4 pass | **1 failed** |
| AudioWorklet: `queuedSamples -= …` in the `handoff` handler (abandoned tail) | 4 pass | **1 failed** |

The other two lines from #30830 (mixed frames retire two samples; the tail discarded at crossfade completion) are already pinned by the merged crossfade cases and stay pinned here.

## Why the merged cases could not see them

Both merged crossfade cases enqueue a new reply at least as long as the crossfade and never issue a second handoff, so the old-only branch is never entered and the `handoff`-time subtraction always runs against an empty handoff queue. The two new cases per backend model the scenarios the lines exist for: a reply shorter than the crossfade, so the old tail plays alone before the queue drains; and a second `beginHandoff` issued mid-crossfade, so the unplayed old samples are discarded with the replaced handoff. Each asserts the depth the sink reports afterwards (`getStats().queuedSamples` on the ScriptProcessor path; the `queue-depth` port message on the worklet path), and the ScriptProcessor old-only case also drives the sink to empty and checks `onDrained` fires once.

## Verification

- `cd packages/ui && bunx vitest run src/voice/__tests__/voice-session-playback.test.ts test/audio-worklets-runtime.test.mjs` — 37/37 at this head
- Each of the four mutants above re-applied at this head: exactly the intended test fails, nothing else
- `biome check` on both files; `packages/ui` typecheck; root `bun run verify`
- Environment: disposable worktree at `origin/develop` (`357084b309`), Bun 1.3.14, `bun install --frozen-lockfile`

## Test-only gate

- **Realistic regression prevented:** a later edit to either backend's crossfade loop or handoff handler that drops one of these retirements reintroduces the #30830 defect for the two scenarios it did not model, with every suite green.
- **Observing consumer:** the playback stats surface — `VoiceSessionPlaybackStats.queuedSamples` / `maxQueuedSamples`, emitted on `finished` and `underrun` through `VoiceSessionClient`'s `onPlaybackStats` — and, on the worklet path, the `queue-depth` messages the sink folds into that surface. An over-count there reports audio that can never play, which is the failure #30830 set out to remove.
- **Why existing coverage does not own it:** demonstrated by mutation above — the merged cases pass with either line deleted in either backend.

Refs: #30830 (merged), #29492.

# Background

## What does this PR do?

`getSetting` returns `string | boolean | number | null` and hands character settings back untouched. The vision config loader compared the raw value for the object and pose toggles to `"true"`, so a boolean `true` in character JSON left both detectors off with no diagnostic, while the face-recognition toggle in the same block already handled the boolean. All three now agree.

## What kind of change is this?

Bug fix.

# Documentation changes needed?

None. The flag names and defaults are unchanged.

45:T764,## Summary

Closes #29569. Supersedes #29570 with the same focused fix rebased onto current `develop`.

LifeOps modules previously passed a file URL's raw `.pathname` into `path.resolve()`. On Windows, a URL such as `file:///C:/repo/...` exposes `/C:/repo/...` as its pathname, which can produce a duplicated drive prefix. Decode the file URL with Node's cross-platform `fileURLToPath()` before resolving it.

The original implementation and commit authorship are preserved from @teddiesloco / #29570. This replacement removes the stale-base and missing-import blockers identified during review.

## Changes

- Decode module-relative repository roots in `env-layers.mjs`, `hitl-ledger.mjs`, and `hitl-credential-dashboard.mjs`.
- Apply the same portable root construction to their affected test helpers.
- Import `fileURLToPath` in every changed module and test.

4b:T7c9,## Summary

Closes #29531. Supersedes #29544 with its focused current-view fix rebased onto current `develop`, plus a correction for declarative-state overmatching.

Natural inspection questions such as `which view is open?` were promoted to the full planner because the heuristic only recognized state adjectives before the view noun. The postpositive grammar now recognizes the question form while remaining anchored to an actual inspection/interrogative token.

## Correctness repair

The earlier postpositive alternative was unanchored and classified ordinary statements such as `the window is open` as inspection. Because `open` inside an inspection span is exempted from action detection, those statements could incorrectly bypass normal routing.

This replacement:

- keeps `which view/window/screen is open/active` read-only;
- requires `identify`, `name`, `tell me`, `what`, or `which` before both grammar forms;
- adds negative controls for declarative state, quoted text, conditionals, and mixed actionable requests;
- retains the existing actionable compound matrix.

Original focused implementation authorship is preserved from @mtcow / #29544.

## Verification

- `git diff --check`: passed.
- Static exact-head audit confirms the postpositive alternative is inside the inspection-token prefix group and cannot begin at a bare view noun.
- Local suite execution is not claimed because this checkout lacks Eliza's dependency closure. Hosted exact-head CI is required before merge.

## Evidence gate

- Backend evidence: focused heuristic regressions included; hosted exact-head logs pending.
- Real-model trajectory: still required before merge for the claimed Cerebras overflow/runtime-routing outcome; this PR does not claim that unit tests prove provider behavior.
- Domain artifacts: N/A.

# Background

## What does this PR do?

Restores `packages/ui/src/cloud/public-pages/pages/login/login-page.same-origin.test.tsx`
to a collectable, passing state.

The suite failed during **vitest collection** — all 4 tests failed to run — with:

```
Error: [vitest] No "STEWARD_TOKEN_KEY" export is defined on the
"@elizaos/shared/steward-session-client" mock. Did you forget to return it from "vi.mock"?
 ❯ src/bridge/storage-bridge.ts:79:3
 ❯ src/state/agent-profiles.ts:9:1
```

Root cause: the login surface's import graph grew to include the storage bridge
(`login-page.tsx` → state/API modules → `src/state/agent-profiles.ts` →
`src/bridge/storage-bridge.ts`). `storage-bridge.ts` imports three members from
`@elizaos/shared/steward-session-client` at **module-evaluation** time:

- `STEWARD_TOKEN_KEY` — read into the native-Preferences sync denylist (line 79),
- `registerStewardTokenPersistence` — called at module init (line 752),
- `registerStewardTokenRemoval` — called at module init (line 751).

The suite's `vi.mock("@elizaos/shared/steward-session-client", …)` factory only
returned `hasStewardAuthedCookie`, `readStoredStewardToken`,
`writeStoredStewardToken`, and `StewardSessionError`. With the three imported
members absent, evaluating `storage-bridge.ts` threw and the whole suite failed
to collect. The suite was green when written and broke when the import graph grew.

The fix extends the existing mock factory with faithful stand-ins verified against
`packages/shared/src/steward-session-client/index.ts`:

- `STEWARD_TOKEN_KEY: "steward_session_token"` — the exact localStorage constant
  (index.ts line 22).
- `registerStewardTokenPersistence: () => () => undefined` — the real function
  (index.ts line ~131) returns an unregister callback; the stand-in mirrors that
  `() => () => void` shape with a no-op.
- `registerStewardTokenRemoval: () => () => undefined` — same, mirroring the real
  registrar at index.ts line ~117.

The mock is kept minimal: only the members the import graph actually consumes are
added, and the four existing test bodies are unchanged.

## What kind of change is this?

Bug fix (test-only): repairs a broken test fixture so the suite collects and runs.

# Documentation changes needed?

My changes do not require a change to the project documentation.

# Testing

## Where should a reviewer start?

factory near line 55).

under Biome.
